The callback spawns N=3 sessions in rapid succession (within ~200ms of each other) via threads. |\n| **Expects** | each session writes its own `state/auggie-first-pending/<sid>.txt` (no cross-contamination); 3 distinct session_init events in 3 separate JSONL files (one per session HOME); `jsonl.event_count == 1` per session. |\n| **Capability tags** | `[]` |\n| **Notes** | Tests no-shared-mutable-state at SessionStart concurrency boundary. Uses the YAML callback escape hatch from D-4 (programmatic spawn ordering can't be expressed in declarative YAML). |\n\n### E15 — Hook timeout fail-open\n\n| Field | Value |\n|---|---|\n| **Inputs** | spawn session with a hooks.json variant where one PostToolUse hook is a fixture script (`tests/fixtures/hooks/slow-post-read.sh`) that sleeps longer than the configured `timeout:` field; inject prompt triggering a Read. |\n| **Expects** | tool call completes successfully (`exit_code.equals(0)`); `duration.less_than(hook_timeout + 2.0)` (harness reaps the slow hook); `jsonl.contains_event(logs/hook-errors.jsonl, type=hook_timeout, disposition=fail_open)`. |\n| **Capability tags** | `[]` |\n| **Notes** | The named failing-case demo from design-spec §11 (\"E15 hook timeout fail-open\"). Hook-surface contract (from `hooks.json` + OQ-2 D-0082 §4)\n52\t\n53\t`src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` SubagentStart block:\n54\t\n55\t```jsonc\n56\t\"SubagentStart\": [\n57\t  {\n58\t    \"hooks\": [\n59\t      { \"type\": \"command\",\n60\t        \"command\": \"~/.claude/hooks/freshness-subagent-start.sh\",\n61\t        \"timeout\": 1,\n62\t        \"async\": true }\n63\t    ]\n64\t  }\n65\t]\n66\t```\n67\t\n68\tNote the absence of a `\"matcher\"` field — SubagentStart has no\n69\tmatcher-group fan-out, so every sub-agent spawn fires the same single\n70\thook command. This mirrors the E3 SessionStart-unmatched and E5\n71\tUserPromptSubmit-unmatched shapes (single-substring assertion, no\n72\tmatcher pin needed).\n73\t\n74\tThe OQ-2 resolution (D-0082 §4 row E10 / decisions.md OQ-2) freezes\n75\tE10's body shape to assert the hook's side-effects per the matched\n76\tbranch:\n77\t\n78\t| Observable | Purpose |\n79\t|---|---|\n80\t| `logs/freshness.jsonl` exists | proves the freshness event ledger was opened by `freshness-subagent-start.sh` (or by a prior SessionStart hook on the same spawn) |\n81\t| `logs/freshness.jsonl` contains `\"type\":\"subagent_start\"` | proves the `subagent_start`-typed event row was emitted to the freshness ledger by `freshness-subagent-start.sh` **after** the async hook flushed |\n82\t| Process exits cleanly on `/quit` | sanity-pin that the spawn lifecycle ran end-to-end (and that the PTY teardown reaped the async writer) |\n83\t\n84\tThese three observables are independently sufficient and discriminate\n85\tthree distinct failure modes: (a) the ledger existence pins that the\n86\tJSONL file was opened; (b) the `subagent_start` substring pins that\n87\tthe SubagentStart hook **emitted its event row** AFTER the async\n88\tbranch flushed; (c) the exit code pins that the session reached clean\n89\tshutdown and the async writer was reaped.\n90\t\n91\tThe single-substring shape (`subagent_start`) differs from the\n92\ttwo-substring shape used by E6 / E7 / E8 (Edit / Write / serena\n93\tmatcher-coverage trio) because the SubagentStart hook has **no\n94\tmatcher pattern** in hooks.json — there is no matcher-group fan-out\n95\tto discriminate. Asserting `\"type\":\"subagent_start\"` is therefore\n96\tsufficient to prove the hook fired; no `\"matcher\":\"...\"` pin is\n97\tneeded (no sibling SubagentStart branches exist).\n98\t\n99\tThe D-0082 §4 row E10 specification phrases the secondary count\n100\tassertion as `jsonl.event_count(logs/freshness.jsonl,\n101\ttype=subagent_start) >= 1`. This predicate requires:\n102\t\n103\t1. Streaming `logs/freshness.jsonl` row-by-row;\n104\t2. Filtering rows where `type == \"subagent_start\"`;\n105\t3. Counting filtered rows and asserting `>= 1`.\n106\t\n107\tNone of these are expressible in the current declarative DSL.\n108\t`Expect.jsonl.event_count` requires a Python callable filter\n109\t(`expect.py:269-369`). Following the same precedent as E3-E9 sibling\n110\tdeferrals (event_count, per-prompt count discrimination), T05.15\n111\tlands the OQ-2 body shape with the binary substring assertion as a\n112\tproxy for the count assertion: if the substring is present at least\n113\tonce in `logs/freshness.jsonl`, then `event_count >= 1` is\n114\tnecessarily true. The strict `>= 1` (vs. `> 0`) form contributes no\n115\tadditional discrimination, so the substring proxy is exact for the\n116\toperationally-meaningful boundary.\n117\t\n118\t## 3. Frozen body shape\n119\t\n120\tThe body lands in `suites/real.yaml` under the E10 entry that\n121\tpreviously carried only stale scaffolding metadata (a placeholder\n122\ttitle `\"installer rejects unknown component class\"` from the\n123\tpre-OQ-2 numbering and no body). This predicate requires a Python\n148\tcallable filter (`expect.py:269-369`) and is not expressible in\n149\tdeclarative YAML. The hook writer is a detached\n316\tbackground process whose flock-protected counter increment (and,\n317\tpost-script-update, freshness ledger append) runs out-of-band. The\n318\t`/quit` teardown reaps the async writer via standard PTY exit-on-EOF:\n319\tthe shell process tree under the PTY is sent SIGHUP, which the async\n320\twriter either completes before receiving (most common case — flock\n321\t+ increment is microseconds) or is killed mid-flight (cleanup path).\n322\t\n323\tE10 inherits the E9 posture: it does NOT assert a specific bound on\n324\tthe flush delay (D-4 callback would be needed for that — deferred\n325\tper §3 footnote). The binary substring presence is the\n326\toperationally-meaningful guarantee: on a host where the async writer\n327\traces teardown, the substring assertion fails and the eval surfaces\n328\tthe regression. On a host where the writer completes pre-teardown\n329\t(the expected case), the substring is present and the eval passes.\n330\t\n331\t### 8.3 Sub-agent lifecycle co-fire considerations\n332\t\n333\tInvoking the Task tool fires three hook events in sequence:\n334\t\n335\t1. **SubagentStart** (this eval's surface) — async, fires when the\n336\t   sub-agent process is spawned.\n337\t2. **(sub-agent runs its work)** — Explore reads files and reports;\n338\t   any internal tool calls fire their own PreToolUse / PostToolUse\n339\t   hooks under the sub-agent's process tree (independent of the\n340\t   parent session's hooks for accounting purposes).\n341\t3. **SubagentStop** — async, fires when the sub-agent returns control\n342\t   to the parent session.\n343\t\n344\tE10 asserts **only** the SubagentStart substring. The SubagentStop\n345\tco-fire is harmless because `Expect.file.contains` requires only that\n346\tthe asserted substring (`\"type\":\"subagent_start\"`) appear somewhere\n347\tin the file — the subagent_stop row coexisting is benign.\n348\t\n349\tE11 (T05.16) will independently assert the paired\n350\t`\"type\":\"subagent_stop\"` substring + the count-equality\n351\t(`event_count(subagent_start) == event_count(subagent_stop)`) — the\n352\tlatter being a Python-callable predicate that follows the same\n353\tdeferral pattern established in §3 footnote.\n354\t\n355\t## 9. Hook-surface contract (from `hooks.json` + OQ-2 D-0082 §4)\n\n`src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` SubagentStart block:\n\n```jsonc\n\"SubagentStart\": [\n  {\n    \"hooks\": [\n      { \"type\": \"command\",\n        \"command\": \"~/.claude/hooks/freshness-subagent-start.sh\",\n        \"timeout\": 1,\n        \"async\": true }\n    ]\n  }\n]\n```\n\nNote the absence of a `\"matcher\"` field — SubagentStart has no\nmatcher-group fan-out, so every sub-agent spawn fires the same single\nhook command. This mirrors the E3 SessionStart-unmatched and E5\nUserPromptSubmit-unmatched shapes (single-substring assertion, no\nmatcher pin needed).\n\nThe OQ-2 resolution (D-0082 §4 row E10 / decisions.md OQ-2) freezes\nE10's body shape to assert the hook's side-effects per the matched\nbranch:\n\n| Observable | Purpose |\n|---|---|\n| `logs/freshness.jsonl` exists | proves the freshness event ledger was opened by `freshness-subagent-start.sh` (or by a prior SessionStart hook on the same spawn) |\n| `logs/freshness.jsonl` contains `\"type\":\"subagent_start\"` | proves the `subagent_start`-typed event row was emitted to the freshness ledger by `freshness-subagent-start.sh` **after** the async hook flushed |\n| Process exits cleanly on `/quit` | sanity-pin that the spawn lifecycle ran end-to-end (and that the PTY teardown reaped the async writer) |\n\nThese three observables are independently sufficient and discriminate\nthree distinct failure modes: (a) the ledger existence pins that the\nJSONL file was opened; (b) the `subagent_start` substring pins that\nthe SubagentStart hook **emitted its event row** AFTER the async\nbranch flushed; (c) the exit code pins that the session reached clean\nshutdown and the async writer was reaped.\n\nThe single-substring shape (`subagent_start`) differs from the\ntwo-substring shape used by E6 / E7 / E8 (Edit / Write / serena\nmatcher-coverage trio) because the SubagentStart hook has **no\nmatcher pattern** in hooks.json — there is no matcher-group fan-out\nto discriminate. Asserting `\"type\":\"subagent_start\"` is therefore\nsufficient to prove the hook fired; no `\"matcher\":\"...\"` pin is\nneeded (no sibling SubagentStart branches exist).\n\nThe D-0082 §4 row E10 specification phrases the secondary count\nassertion as `jsonl.event_count(logs/freshness.jsonl,\ntype=subagent_start) >= 1`. This predicate requires:\n\n1. Streaming `logs/freshness.jsonl` row-by-row;\n2. Filtering rows where `type == \"subagent_start\"`;\n3. Counting filtered rows and asserting `>= 1`.\n\nNone of these are expressible in the current declarative DSL.\n`Expect.jsonl.event_count` requires a Python callable filter\n(`expect.py:269-369`). Following the same precedent as E3-E9 sibling\ndeferrals (event_count, per-prompt count discrimination), T05.15\nlands the OQ-2 body shape with the binary substring assertion as a\nproxy for the count assertion: if the substring is present at least\nonce in `logs/freshness.jsonl`, then `event_count >= 1` is\nnecessarily true. The strict `>= 1` (vs. `> 0`) form contributes no\nadditional discrimination, so the substring proxy is exact for the\noperationally-meaningful boundary.\n\n## 3. The script does NOT write to\n`logs/freshness.jsonl` and does NOT emit a JSONL envelope with a\n`type` field.\n\nThis gap is **not introduced** by T05.15; it predates the\ndeliverable and mirrors the identical telemetry gaps discovered\nfor `session-init.sh` during T05.07 (D-0087 §8.1),\n`freshness-session-start.sh` during T05.08 (D-0088 §8.1),\n`freshness-user-prompt.sh` during T05.09 (D-0089 §8.1),\n`freshness-pre-edit.sh` during T05.10 / T05.11 / T05.13\n(D-0090/D-0091/D-0092 §8.1), and `freshness-post-read.sh` during\nT05.14 (D-0093 §8.1). E10 stands alone in script terms (no sibling\nshares `freshness-subagent-start.sh`); the paired\n`freshness-subagent-stop.sh` (E11) is structurally analogous but a\ndistinct script, so the script-update follow-up will land two\nupdates (one per script) for the SubagentStart/SubagentStop pair.\n\nAcceptance criteria for T05.15 (manifest body landed, FR-SCH2-valid\nid, OQ-2 body shape recorded, spec/notes/evidence written) are met\nby the describe / list / roundtrip evidence above; the per-task AC\nthat requires `eval run --eval E10` to exit 0 deterministically\ndepends transitively on (a) the runner NameError fix, and (b) the\nfreshness-subagent-start.sh emit-observables update with the OQ-2\ncontract path + field name.\n\n### 8.2 Async-branch reaping under PTY teardown\n\nBecause the matched hook is `async: true` (same as E9's PostToolUse\nRead), the Claude Code runtime does NOT block on the hook's exit\nbefore continuing the sub-agent spawn. The hook writer is a detached\nbackground process whose flock-protected counter increment (and,\npost-script-update, freshness ledger append) runs out-of-band. The\n`/quit` teardown reaps the async writer via standard PTY exit-on-EOF:\nthe shell process tree under the PTY is sent SIGHUP, which the async\nwriter either completes before receiving (most common case — flock\n+ increment is microseconds) or is killed mid-flight (cleanup path).\n\nE10 inherits the E9 posture: it does NOT assert a specific bound on\nthe flush delay (D-4 callback would be needed for that — deferred\nper §3 footnote). The binary substring presence is the\noperationally-meaningful guarantee: on a host where the async writer\nraces teardown, the substring assertion fails and the eval surfaces\nthe regression. On a host where the writer completes pre-teardown\n(the expected case), the substring is present and the eval passes.\n\n### 8.3 Sub-agent lifecycle co-fire considerations\n\nInvoking the Task tool fires three hook events in sequence:\n\n1. **SubagentStart** (this eval's surface) — async, fires when the\n   sub-agent process is spawned.\n2. **(sub-agent runs its work)** — Explore reads files and reports;\n   any internal tool calls fire their own PreToolUse / PostToolUse\n   hooks under the sub-agent's process tree (independent of the\n   parent session's hooks for accounting purposes).\n3. **SubagentStop** — async, fires when the sub-agent returns control\n   to the parent session.\n\nE10 asserts **only** the SubagentStart substring. The SubagentStop\nco-fire is harmless because `Expect.file.contains` requires only that\nthe asserted substring (`\"type\":\"subagent_start\"`) appear somewhere\nin the file — the subagent_stop row coexisting is benign.\n\nE11 (T05.16) will independently assert the paired\n`\"type\":\"subagent_stop\"` substring + the count-equality\n(`event_count(subagent_start) == event_count(subagent_stop)`) — the\nlatter being a Python-callable predicate that follows the same\ndeferral pattern established in §3 footnote.\n\n## 9. The adapter performs this\n   second copy itself.\n\nDefense-in-depth: a pre-flight refusal asserts ``home_path`` is not the\nreal ``~/.claude/`` (resolves both sides; rejects any equality or\ndescendant relationship). This complements but does not replace the\n:class:`HomeIsolation` containment guard — the adapter MAY be invoked\ndirectly by tests, so the guard must live here too.\n\nIdempotency: re-invoking ``deploy_hooks_to`` against the same\n``home_path`` produces identical filesystem state. ``install_hooks``\nitself is idempotent (existing scripts are skipped without ``--force``;\nthe settings.json merge skips matcher collisions). The verbatim\n``hooks.json`` copy uses ``shutil.copy2`` which overwrites with the same\nbytes when the source is unchanged.\n\nError contract: any failure inside the adapter raises\n:class:`HookDeployFailed` with an :attr:`HookDeployFailed.error_tag`\nattribute the EvalRunner (T03.x) propagates into\n``EvalRunner.outcome.artifacts``. The tag is a short kebab-case\nidentifier the orchestrator buckets ERRORED evals by without parsing\nhuman-readable strings.\n\nCross-links\n===========\n\n* COMP-006 :class:`HomeIsolation` (T02.11 / D-0032) — supplies the\n  ``home_path`` argument.\n* NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup wrapper (T02.13 / D-0033) — invokes the\n  adapter from inside ``HomeIsolation.setup``'s post-mkdtemp block in\n  a future wiring task.\n* :func:`superclaude.cli.install_hooks.install_hooks` — the underlying\n  installer this adapter wraps.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport shutil\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom superclaude.cli.install_hooks import install_hooks\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"HookDeployFailed\",\n    \"deploy_hooks_to\",\n]\n\n\n# Relative paths under the per-eval HOME that the adapter writes to.\n_SETTINGS_RELPATH = (\".claude\", \"settings.json\")\n_HOOKS_JSON_RELPATH = (\".claude\", \"hooks.json\")\n\n\nclass HookDeployFailed(Exception):\n    \"\"\"Raised by :func:`deploy_hooks_to` when hook deployment cannot complete.\n\n    Carries a short kebab-case :attr:`error_tag` the orchestrator\n    (:class:`EvalRunner`, T03.x) routes into\n    ``EvalRunner.outcome.artifacts`` so failed evals can be bucketed\n    without parsing the human-readable message.\n\n    Attributes:\n        error_tag: Short identifier for the failure mode. One of:\n\n            * ``\"refused-real-home\"`` — ``home_path`` resolved to (or\n              under) the real ``~/.claude/``; defense-in-depth guard\n              fired BEFORE any filesystem write.\n            * ``\"install-hooks-failed\"`` — the underlying\n              :func:`install_hooks` returned ``success=False``. The\n              installer's message is preserved in :attr:`detail`.\n            * ``\"hooks-json-copy-failed\"`` — the verbatim\n              ``hooks.json`` copy raised :class:`OSError`. The OS\n              error message is preserved in :attr:`detail`.\n            * ``\"source-hooks-json-missing\"`` — the source\n              ``src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json`` is not on disk.\n              Should never happen in a working checkout; surfaced\n              explicitly to make missing-source bugs loud.\n\n        home_path: The per-eval HOME the adapter was asked to deploy to.\n        detail: Human-readable failure detail (installer message,\n            ``OSError`` rendering, etc.). Like E9, the matched hook is\n23\tdeclared `async: true`, so the body relies on PTY teardown reaping\n24\tthe async writer with the binary substring presence as the\n25\toperationally-meaningful guarantee that the hook fired and flushed\n26\tbefore session exit.\n27\t\n28\tE10 also opens the **sub-agent lifecycle pair**: E10 covers\n29\tSubagentStart firing; E11 (D-0095 / T05.16) covers the matched\n30\tSubagentStop firing with `event_count(subagent_start) ==\n31\tevent_count(subagent_stop)` symmetry. Hook-surface contract (from `hooks.json` + OQ-2 D-0082 §4)\n52\t\n53\t`src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` SubagentStart block:\n54\t\n55\t```jsonc\n56\t\"SubagentStart\": [\n57\t  {\n58\t    \"hooks\": [\n59\t      { \"type\": \"command\",\n60\t        \"command\": \"~/.claude/hooks/freshness-subagent-start.sh\",\n61\t        \"timeout\": 1,\n62\t        \"async\": true }\n63\t    ]\n64\t  }\n65\t]\n66\t```\n67\t\n68\tNote the absence of a `\"matcher\"` field — SubagentStart has no\n69\tmatcher-group fan-out, so every sub-agent spawn fires the same single\n70\thook command. This mirrors the E3 SessionStart-unmatched and E5\n71\tUserPromptSubmit-unmatched shapes (single-substring assertion, no\n72\tmatcher pin needed).\n73\t\n74\tThe OQ-2 resolution (D-0082 §4 row E10 / decisions.md OQ-2) freezes\n75\tE10's body shape to assert the hook's side-effects per the matched\n76\tbranch:\n77\t\n78\t| Observable | Purpose |\n79\t|---|---|\n80\t| `logs/freshness.jsonl` exists | proves the freshness event ledger was opened by `freshness-subagent-start.sh` (or by a prior SessionStart hook on the same spawn) |\n81\t| `logs/freshness.jsonl` contains `\"type\":\"subagent_start\"` | proves the `subagent_start`-typed event row was emitted to the freshness ledger by `freshness-subagent-start.sh` **after** the async hook flushed |\n82\t| Process exits cleanly on `/quit` | sanity-pin that the spawn lifecycle ran end-to-end (and that the PTY teardown reaped the async writer) |\n83\t\n84\tThese three observables are independently sufficient and discriminate\n85\tthree distinct failure modes: (a) the ledger existence pins that the\n86\tJSONL file was opened; (b) the `subagent_start` substring pins that\n87\tthe SubagentStart hook **emitted its event row** AFTER the async\n88\tbranch flushed; (c) the exit code pins that the session reached clean\n89\tshutdown and the async writer was reaped.\n90\t\n91\tThe single-substring shape (`subagent_start`) differs from the\n92\ttwo-substring shape used by E6 / E7 / E8 (Edit / Write / serena\n93\tmatcher-coverage trio) because the SubagentStart hook has **no\n94\tmatcher pattern** in hooks.json — there is no matcher-group fan-out\n95\tto discriminate. Asserting `\"type\":\"subagent_start\"` is therefore\n96\tsufficient to prove the hook fired; no `\"matcher\":\"...\"` pin is\n97\tneeded (no sibling SubagentStart branches exist).\n98\t\n99\tThe D-0082 §4 row E10 specification phrases the secondary count\n100\tassertion as `jsonl.event_count(logs/freshness.jsonl,\n101\ttype=subagent_start) >= 1`. This predicate requires:\n102\t\n103\t1. Streaming `logs/freshness.jsonl` row-by-row;\n104\t2. Filtering rows where `type == \"subagent_start\"`;\n105\t3. Counting filtered rows and asserting `>= 1`.\n106\t\n107\tNone of these are expressible in the current declarative DSL.\n108\t`Expect.jsonl.event_count` requires a Python callable filter\n109\t(`expect.py:269-369`). Following the same precedent as E3-E9 sibling\n110\tdeferrals (event_count, per-prompt count discrimination), T05.15\n111\tlands the OQ-2 body shape with the binary substring assertion as a\n112\tproxy for the count assertion: if the substring is present at least\n113\tonce in `logs/freshness.jsonl`, then `event_count >= 1` is\n114\tnecessarily true. The strict `>= 1` (vs. `> 0`) form contributes no\n115\tadditional discrimination, so the substring proxy is exact for the\n116\toperationally-meaningful boundary.\n117\t\n118\t## 3. This predicate requires a Python\n148\tcallable filter (`expect.py:269-369`) and is not expressible in\n149\tdeclarative YAML. Following the same precedent as E3-E9 sibling\n150\tdeferrals, T05.15 lands the OQ-2 body shape with the binary\n151\tsubstring assertion as the operational proxy. The substring is\n152\tpresent iff at least one row carries `\"type\":\"subagent_start\"`, so\n153\tthe `>= 1` boundary is exactly preserved by the proxy. Determinism (3-run AC)\n177\t\n178\tThe body is deterministic on a clean per-eval HOME (D-0082 §2\n179\tconstraint 2 / per-task AC):\n180\t\n181\t- The PTY spawn writes a new freshness ledger entry on every\n182\t  SubagentStart fire (FR-ISO2 fresh HOME per eval — no carry-over).\n183\t- The Task tool invocation triggers exactly one SubagentStart event\n184\t  per the single Task prompt; the async hook emits one\n185\t  `subagent_start`-typed counter increment (post-script-update, one\n186\t  JSONL row with `\"type\":\"subagent_start\"`).\n187\t- The chosen sub-agent task (glob `*.md` under cwd) is read-only,\n188\t  deterministic, and bounded — Explore will enumerate the per-eval\n189\t  HOME's markdown files (typically zero on a clean ephemeral HOME)\n190\t  and report the count, then return. No external network calls; no\n191\t  shared-state dependencies.\n192\t- The `/quit` input causes an immediate clean exit (exit code 0)\n193\t  after the async writer reaps.\n194\t- No time-of-day, network, or shared-state dependencies — D-0082 §2\n195\t  constraint 3 (no `CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`) is honored. The `ts`,\n196\t  `session_id`, agent-type / agent-instance fields on the\n197\t  (post-update) JSONL row are not asserted against.\n198\t\n199\tThree consecutive `eval run --suite real --eval E10` invocations on\n200\ta clean HOME must therefore yield identical EvalOutcome statuses,\n201\twhich is the per-task acceptance criterion.\n202\t\n203\t## 6. Schema validation\n204\t\n205\tThe body uses only manifest-supported constructs:\n206\t\n207\t- `inputs[].prompt: string` (additionalProperties: true under\n208\t  `evalEntry.inputs.items` per `suite.schema.json`). The two-element\n209\t  `inputs[]` array is accepted by the open-shape array schema. The\n210\t  `expect_tool_call` field on `inputs[0]` is accepted by the\n211\t  open-shape (mirrors E1 / E2.1-3 / E8 / E9 usage).\n212\t- `expects[]` rows matching `{primitive: kwargs}` shape — resolved at\n213\t  load-time by `Expect.from_mapping` (`expect.py:640-669`). All three\n214\t  primitives used (2×`file`, 1×`exit_code`) are in `PRIMITIVE_NAMES`\n215\t  (`expect.py:56-64`).\n216\t- `file` primitive kwargs `path`, `exists`, `contains` are supported\n217\t  by `Expect.file._build` (`expect.py:186-265`).\n218\t- `exit_code` primitive kwarg `equals` is supported by\n219\t  `Expect.exit_code._build`.\n220\t- `requires: []` (empty / omitted) is accepted by the schema.\n221\t- `timeout_sec: 60` is `integer ≥ 1` per schema.\n222\t- `isolation.home_strategy: ephemeral` is in the enum.\n223\t- `no_pty: skip` matches the enum.\n224\t\n225\tNo schema-version bump required.\n226\t\n227\t## 7. `--no-mcp` and `--no-pty` behavior matrix\n228\t\n229\t| Invocation | E10 outcome | Why |\n230\t|---|---|---|\n231\t| `eval run --suite real --eval E10` (no flags) | RUNS | no capability tags; PTY harness present on host |\n232\t| `eval run --suite real --eval E10 --no-mcp` | RUNS | `requires: []` — no MCP capability to skip; Task tool is built-in |\n233\t| `eval run --suite real --eval E10 --no-pty` | SKIPPED (`--no-pty`) | per-eval `no_pty: skip` tag (R-077 / D-0077); `--no-pty` short-circuits before any eval body executes |\n234\t| `eval run --suite real --eval E10 --no-mcp --no-pty` | SKIPPED (`--no-pty`) | `--no-pty` short-circuits first per `commands.py` |\n235\t\n236\tThis posture matches siblings E3-E7 / E9 (which also carry\n237\t`requires: []`) and differs from E1 / E2.1-3 / E8 (which carry MCP\n238\tcapability tags and soft-skip under `--no-mcp`).\n239\t\n240\t## 8. Purpose\n\nAuthor the **inputs + expects** body for eval E10 — the eighth of the\npost-OQ-2 hook-event coverage entries (R-086 … R-098). E10 covers the\n**SubagentStart hook** in `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` — whose\ncommand is `freshness-subagent-start.sh` (timeout=1, `async: true`,\nno matcher).\n\nE10 is the **second async hook eval** in the roster — after E9\n(D-0093 / PostToolUse Read async). Like E9, the matched hook is\ndeclared `async: true`, so the body relies on PTY teardown reaping\nthe async writer with the binary substring presence as the\noperationally-meaningful guarantee that the hook fired and flushed\nbefore session exit.\n\nE10 also opens the **sub-agent lifecycle pair**: E10 covers\nSubagentStart firing; E11 (D-0095 / T05.16) covers the matched\nSubagentStop firing with `event_count(subagent_start) ==\nevent_count(subagent_stop)` symmetry. The two evals are designed to\nbe sibling pins on the sub-agent lifecycle and share the input shape\n(spawn a sub-agent via the Task tool) while asserting different\nhook-event substrings.\n\nThe body must:\n\n- spawn a fresh claude session via the PTY harness, inject a prompt\n  that invokes a sub-agent via the Task tool (`subagent_type='Explore'`\n  per D-0082 §4 \"e.g., Explore or Plan\"), then exit cleanly so the\n  PTY teardown reaps both the sub-agent and the async hook writer;\n- assert the hook's observable side-effects (freshness ledger present\n  + `subagent_start` event row);\n- exit cleanly so the `exit_code.equals(0)` assertion can pin a clean\n  `/quit` — and so the PTY teardown reaps the async writer (sibling\n  pattern to E9);\n- carry no capability tag (`requires: []`) — the Task / Agent tool is\n  a built-in Claude Code tool, not an MCP tool, so E10 runs on every\n  host and under `--no-mcp`.\n\n## 2. Hook-surface contract (from `hooks.json` + OQ-2 D-0082 §4)\n\n`src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` SubagentStart block:\n\n```jsonc\n\"SubagentStart\": [\n  {\n    \"hooks\": [\n      { \"type\": \"command\",\n        \"command\": \"~/.claude/hooks/freshness-subagent-start.sh\",\n        \"timeout\": 1,\n        \"async\": true }\n    ]\n  }\n]\n```\n\nNote the absence of a `\"matcher\"` field — SubagentStart has no\nmatcher-group fan-out, so every sub-agent spawn fires the same single\nhook command. This mirrors the E3 SessionStart-unmatched and E5\nUserPromptSubmit-unmatched shapes (single-substring assertion, no\nmatcher pin needed).\n\nThe OQ-2 resolution (D-0082 §4 row E10 / decisions.md OQ-2) freezes\nE10's body shape to assert the hook's side-effects per the matched\nbranch:\n\n| Observable | Purpose |\n|---|---|\n| `logs/freshness.jsonl` exists | proves the freshness event ledger was opened by `freshness-subagent-start.sh` (or by a prior SessionStart hook on the same spawn) |\n| `logs/freshness.jsonl` contains `\"type\":\"subagent_start\"` | proves the `subagent_start`-typed event row was emitted to the freshness ledger by `freshness-subagent-start.sh` **after** the async hook flushed |\n| Process exits cleanly on `/quit` | sanity-pin that the spawn lifecycle ran end-to-end (and that the PTY teardown reaped the async writer) |\n\nThese three observables are independently sufficient and discriminate\nthree distinct failure modes: (a) the ledger existence pins that the\nJSONL file was opened; (b) the `subagent_start` substring pins that\nthe SubagentStart hook **emitted its event row** AFTER the async\nbranch flushed; (c) the exit code pins that the session reached clean\nshutdown and the async writer was reaped.\n\nThe single-substring shape (`subagent_start`) differs from the\ntwo-substring shape used by E6 / E7 / E8 (Edit / Write / serena\nmatcher-coverage trio) because the SubagentStart hook has **no\nmatcher pattern** in hooks.json — there is no matcher-group fan-out\nto discriminate. Asserting `\"type\":\"subagent_start\"` is therefore\nsufficient to prove the hook fired; no `\"matcher\":\"...\"` pin is\nneeded (no sibling SubagentStart branches exist).\n\nThe D-0082 §4 row E10 specification phrases the secondary count\nassertion as `jsonl.event_count(logs/freshness.jsonl,\ntype=subagent_start) >= 1`. This predicate requires:\n\n1. Streaming `logs/freshness.jsonl` row-by-row;\n2. Filtering rows where `type == \"subagent_start\"`;\n3. Counting filtered rows and asserting `>= 1`.\n\nNone of these are expressible in the current declarative DSL.\n`Expect.jsonl.event_count` requires a Python callable filter\n(`expect.py:269-369`). Following the same precedent as E3-E9 sibling\ndeferrals (event_count, per-prompt count discrimination), T05.15\nlands the OQ-2 body shape with the binary substring assertion as a\nproxy for the count assertion: if the substring is present at least\nonce in `logs/freshness.jsonl`, then `event_count >= 1` is\nnecessarily true. The strict `>= 1` (vs. `> 0`) form contributes no\nadditional discrimination, so the substring proxy is exact for the\noperationally-meaningful boundary.\n\n## 3. Frozen body shape\n\nThe body lands in `suites/real.yaml` under the E10 entry that\npreviously carried only stale scaffolding metadata (a placeholder\ntitle `\"installer rejects unknown component class\"` from the\npre-OQ-2 numbering and no body). T05.15 replaces the scaffolding\nwith the frozen body. Final shape:\n\n| Field | Value |\n|---|---|\n| **title** | `\"SubagentStart hook fires\"` (matches D-0082 §4 OQ-2 row E10) |\n| **category** | `hook-lifecycle` (sibling to E3-E9; was `installer` in stale stub) |\n| **requires** | `[]` — no capability tags; Task / Agent tool is a built-in Claude Code tool, no MCP needed |\n| **timeout_sec** | `60` (raised from defaults' 120s — two-prompt PTY round-trip is bounded by the Task tool round-trip + 1s async flush, typically <30s on a lightweight Explore-glob task; 60s is generous; matches E3/E4/E5/E6/E7/E8/E9 sibling for spawn-lifecycle parity) |\n| **inputs[0].prompt** | Task fire: `\"Use the Task tool with subagent_type='Explore' to find files matching '*.md' under the current working directory and report the count.\"` + `expect_tool_call: Task` — the Task invocation that spawns the Explore sub-agent and fires the SubagentStart hook |\n| **inputs[1].prompt** | `\"/quit\"` — clean session exit so `exit_code.equals(0)` can pin the PTY teardown contract AND so the teardown reaps the async writer (and any in-flight sub-agent) |\n| **expects[0]** | `file: { path: logs/freshness.jsonl, exists: true }` |\n| **expects[1]** | `file: { path: logs/freshness.jsonl, exists: true, contains: '\"type\":\"subagent_start\"' }` |\n| **expects[2]** | `exit_code: { equals: 0 }` |\n\nPTY-exclusion tag: `no_pty: skip` (carried forward from the\nscaffolding entry — every eval in the `real` suite is PTY-driven\nper DOC-OQ3 / R-077).\n\nNo additions to `optional_capabilities` — Task / Agent is built into\nClaude Code; no MCP server is required.\n\n**Footnote — event_count deferral.** D-0082 §4 row E10 lists a\nsecondary assertion `jsonl.event_count(logs/freshness.jsonl,\ntype=subagent_start) >= 1`. This predicate requires a Python\ncallable filter (`expect.py:269-369`) and is not expressible in\ndeclarative YAML. Following the same precedent as E3-E9 sibling\ndeferrals, T05.15 lands the OQ-2 body shape with the binary\nsubstring assertion as the operational proxy. The substring is\npresent iff at least one row carries `\"type\":\"subagent_start\"`, so\nthe `>= 1` boundary is exactly preserved by the proxy. The strict\ndeclarative form remains deferred until either (a) the YAML callback\nescape hatch (D-4) is exercised for E10, or (b) a future schema bump\nadds a declarative `jsonl: { contains_event: { type: ... } }`\nshorthand. Neither is in scope for T05.15.\n\nNote: invoking the Task tool fires the SubagentStart hook before the\nsub-agent begins, and the SubagentStop hook after the sub-agent\nreturns. E10 only asserts the subagent_start substring; E11 (T05.16)\nwill independently assert the paired subagent_stop substring. Determinism (3-run AC)\n\nThe body is deterministic on a clean per-eval HOME (D-0082 §2\nconstraint 2 / per-task AC):\n\n- The PTY spawn writes a new freshness ledger entry on every\n  SubagentStart fire (FR-ISO2 fresh HOME per eval — no carry-over).\n- The Task tool invocation triggers exactly one SubagentStart event\n  per the single Task prompt; the async hook emits one\n  `subagent_start`-typed counter increment (post-script-update, one\n  JSONL row with `\"type\":\"subagent_start\"`).\n- The chosen sub-agent task (glob `*.md` under cwd) is read-only,\n  deterministic, and bounded — Explore will enumerate the per-eval\n  HOME's markdown files (typically zero on a clean ephemeral HOME)\n  and report the count, then return. No external network calls; no\n  shared-state dependencies.\n- The `/quit` input causes an immediate clean exit (exit code 0)\n  after the async writer reaps.\n- No time-of-day, network, or shared-state dependencies — D-0082 §2\n  constraint 3 (no `CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`) is honored. The `ts`,\n  `session_id`, agent-type / agent-instance fields on the\n  (post-update) JSONL row are not asserted against.\n\nThree consecutive `eval run --suite real --eval E10` invocations on\na clean HOME must therefore yield identical EvalOutcome statuses,\nwhich is the per-task acceptance criterion.\n\n## 6. The hook writer is a detached\nbackground process whose flock-protected counter increment (and,\npost-script-update, freshness ledger append) runs out-of-band. The\n`/quit` teardown reaps the async writer via standard PTY exit-on-EOF:\nthe shell process tree under the PTY is sent SIGHUP, which the async\nwriter either completes before receiving (most common case — flock\n+ increment is microseconds) or is killed mid-flight (cleanup path).\n\nE10 inherits the E9 posture: it does NOT assert a specific bound on\nthe flush delay (D-4 callback would be needed for that — deferred\nper §3 footnote). The binary substring presence is the\noperationally-meaningful guarantee: on a host where the async writer\nraces teardown, the substring assertion fails and the eval surfaces\nthe regression. On a host where the writer completes pre-teardown\n(the expected case), the substring is present and the eval passes.\n\n### 8.3 Sub-agent lifecycle co-fire considerations\n\nInvoking the Task tool fires three hook events in sequence:\n\n1. **SubagentStart** (this eval's surface) — async, fires when the\n   sub-agent process is spawned.\n2. **(sub-agent runs its work)** — Explore reads files and reports;\n   any internal tool calls fire their own PreToolUse / PostToolUse\n   hooks under the sub-agent's process tree (independent of the\n   parent session's hooks for accounting purposes).\n3. **SubagentStop** — async, fires when the sub-agent returns control\n   to the parent session.\n\nE10 asserts **only** the SubagentStart substring. The SubagentStop\nco-fire is harmless because `Expect.file.contains` requires only that\nthe asserted substring (`\"type\":\"subagent_start\"`) appear somewhere\nin the file — the subagent_stop row coexisting is benign.\n\nE11 (T05.16) will independently assert the paired\n`\"type\":\"subagent_stop\"` substring + the count-equality\n(`event_count(subagent_start) == event_count(subagent_stop)`) — the\nlatter being a Python-callable predicate that follows the same\ndeferral pattern established in §3 footnote.\n\n## 9. Impacts / dependencies\n\n| Direction | Item | Note |\n|---|---|---|\n| Depends on | T05.01 / D-0082 | OQ-2 resolution — frozen body shape |\n| Depends on | T01.05 (FR-SCH2 validate_eval_id) | accepts literal `E10` |\n| Depends on | T04.02 / T04.03 (Expect.file impl) | satisfied by current `expect.py` |\n| Depends on | T04.04 / T04.05 (Expect.exit_code impl) | satisfied by current `expect.py` |\n| Sibling | T05.07 (E3 body) | first-position SessionStart hook (no-matcher single-substring template — same shape as E10) |\n| Sibling | T05.09 (E5 body) | UserPromptSubmit no-matcher hook (no-matcher single-substring template — same shape as E10) |\n| Sibling | T05.14 (E9 body) | PostToolUse Read async hook (first async hook eval; established the PTY-teardown reap pattern that E10 follows) |\n| Sibling | T05.16 (E11 body) | SubagentStop hook — paired with E10 on sub-agent lifecycle; will assert subagent_stop substring + count-equality predicate |\n| Unblocks | T05.16 (E11 body) | E11 follows the no-matcher async-hook body template established here |\n| Unblocks | T05.18 (CP-P05-T13-T17 checkpoint) | E10 must enumerate + describe; full-run verification follows runner fix |\n\n## 10. Used by the\n122\t    defense-in-depth refusal so a per-eval ``home_path`` that is a\n123\t    symlink to the real user HOME still fails the equality / descendant\n124\t    check.\n125\t    \"\"\"\n126\t\n127\t    return (Path.home() / \".claude\").resolve(strict=False)\n128\t\n129\t\n130\tdef _refuse_real_home(home_path: Path) -> None:\n131\t    \"\"\"Reject ``home_path`` when any adapter write would land under real ``~/.claude/``.\n132\t\n133\t    Two attack surfaces are caught:\n134\t\n135\t    * ``home_path`` itself resolves to (or under) the real\n136\t      ``~/.claude/`` directory — every adapter write is anchored at\n137\t      ``home_path``, so this catches direct attempts.\n138\t    * ``home_path / .claude`` resolves to (or under) the real\n139\t      ``~/.claude/`` — the subtle case where the caller passes their\n140\t      own ``$HOME`` as ``home_path`` and the adapter's\n141\t      ``<home_path>/.claude/settings.json`` write would otherwise\n142\t      silently target the real configuration directory.\n143\t\n144\t    Both sides are resolved with ``strict=False`` so a symlink in either\n145\t    direction is collapsed before comparison.\n146\t    \"\"\"\n147\t\n148\t    real = _real_user_claude_dir()\n149\t    resolved_home = home_path.resolve(strict=False)\n150\t    resolved_claude_subdir = (home_path / \".claude\").resolve(strict=False)\n151\t\n152\t    def _matches(candidate: Path) -> bool:\n153\t        return candidate == real or candidate.is_relative_to(real)\n154\t\n155\t    if _matches(resolved_home) or _matches(resolved_claude_subdir):\n156\t        raise HookDeployFailed(\n157\t            error_tag=\"refused-real-home\",\n158\t            home_path=home_path,\n159\t            detail=(\n160\t                f\"home_path {home_path!s} (resolved={resolved_home!s}; A separate copy\n   pipeline would drift; the COMP-014 reuse contract pins this to the\n   one installer.\n2. **Deploy ``hooks.json`` verbatim.** ``install_hooks`` merges hook\n   registrations *into* ``settings.json`` but never copies the source\n   ``hooks.json`` to the target HOME. The acceptance criteria for T02.14\n   require ``<home_path>/.claude/hooks.json`` to be byte-identical to\n   ``src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json`` so post-mortem inspection and the\n   FR-G* gate tests can SHA256-compare the source-of-truth registrations\n   against what the per-eval HOME shipped. The adapter performs this\n   second copy itself.\n\nDefense-in-depth: a pre-flight refusal asserts ``home_path`` is not the\nreal ``~/.claude/`` (resolves both sides; rejects any equality or\ndescendant relationship). This complements but does not replace the\n:class:`HomeIsolation` containment guard — the adapter MAY be invoked\ndirectly by tests, so the guard must live here too.\n\nIdempotency: re-invoking ``deploy_hooks_to`` against the same\n``home_path`` produces identical filesystem state. ``install_hooks``\nitself is idempotent (existing scripts are skipped without ``--force``;\nthe settings.json merge skips matcher collisions). The verbatim\n``hooks.json`` copy uses ``shutil.copy2`` which overwrites with the same\nbytes when the source is unchanged.\n\nError contract: any failure inside the adapter raises\n:class:`HookDeployFailed` with an :attr:`HookDeployFailed.error_tag`\nattribute the EvalRunner (T03.x) propagates into\n``EvalRunner.outcome.artifacts``. The tag is a short kebab-case\nidentifier the orchestrator buckets ERRORED evals by without parsing\nhuman-readable strings.\n\nCross-links\n===========\n\n* COMP-006 :class:`HomeIsolation` (T02.11 / D-0032) — supplies the\n  ``home_path`` argument.\n* NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup wrapper (T02.13 / D-0033) — invokes the\n  adapter from inside ``HomeIsolation.setup``'s post-mkdtemp block in\n  a future wiring task.\n* :func:`superclaude.cli.install_hooks.install_hooks` — the underlying\n  installer this adapter wraps.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport shutil\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom superclaude.cli.install_hooks import install_hooks\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"HookDeployFailed\",\n    \"deploy_hooks_to\",\n]\n\n\n# Relative paths under the per-eval HOME that the adapter writes to.\n_SETTINGS_RELPATH = (\".claude\", \"settings.json\")\n_HOOKS_JSON_RELPATH = (\".claude\", \"hooks.json\")\n\n\nclass HookDeployFailed(Exception):\n    \"\"\"Raised by :func:`deploy_hooks_to` when hook deployment cannot complete.\n\n    Carries a short kebab-case :attr:`error_tag` the orchestrator\n    (:class:`EvalRunner`, T03.x) routes into\n    ``EvalRunner.outcome.artifacts`` so failed evals can be bucketed\n    without parsing the human-readable message.\n\n    Attributes:\n        error_tag: Short identifier for the failure mode. The\n              installer's message is preserved in :attr:`detail`.\n            * ``\"hooks-json-copy-failed\"`` — the verbatim\n              ``hooks.json`` copy raised :class:`OSError`. The OS\n              error message is preserved in :attr:`detail`.\n            * ``\"source-hooks-json-missing\"`` — the source\n              ``src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json`` is not on disk.\n              Should never happen in a working checkout; surfaced\n              explicitly to make missing-source bugs loud.\n\n        home_path: The per-eval HOME the adapter was asked to deploy to.\n        detail: Human-readable failure detail (installer message,\n            ``OSError`` rendering, etc.). Never contains the error_tag\n            itself so the two surfaces stay independent.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        *,\n        error_tag: str,\n        home_path: Path,\n        detail: str,\n    ) -> None:\n        self.error_tag = error_tag\n        self.home_path = home_path\n        self.detail = detail\n        super().__init__(\n            f\"COMP-014 hook deploy failed [{error_tag}] for home_path={home_path!s}: {detail}\"\n        )\n\n\ndef _real_user_claude_dir() -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Resolve the user's real ``~/.claude/`` directory.\n\n    Returns the resolved (symlinks collapsed) path. Used by the\n    defense-in-depth refusal so a per-eval ``home_path`` that is a\n    symlink to the real user HOME still fails the equality / descendant\n    check.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    return (Path.home() / \".claude\").resolve(strict=False)\n\n\ndef _refuse_real_home(home_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Reject ``home_path`` when any adapter write would land under real ``~/.claude/``.\n\n    Two attack surfaces are caught:\n\n    * ``home_path`` itself resolves to (or under) the real\n      ``~/.claude/`` directory — every adapter write is anchored at\n      ``home_path``, so this catches direct attempts.\n    * ``home_path / .claude`` resolves to (or under) the real\n      ``~/.claude/`` — the subtle case where the caller passes their\n      own ``$HOME`` as ``home_path`` and the adapter's\n      ``<home_path>/.claude/settings.json`` write would otherwise\n      silently target the real configuration directory.\n\n    Both sides are resolved with ``strict=False`` so a symlink in either\n    direction is collapsed before comparison.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    real = _real_user_claude_dir()\n    resolved_home = home_path.resolve(strict=False)\n    resolved_claude_subdir = (home_path / \".claude\").resolve(strict=False)\n\n    def _matches(candidate: Path) -> bool:\n        return candidate == real or candidate.is_relative_to(real)\n\n    if _matches(resolved_home) or _matches(resolved_claude_subdir):\n        raise HookDeployFailed(\n            error_tag=\"refused-real-home\",\n            home_path=home_path,\n            detail=(\n                f\"home_path {home_path!s} (resolved={resolved_home!s}; This\n41\t   keeps the eval body free of unintended PreToolUse co-fires from\n42\t   the sub-agent's own tool calls (Plan can call any tool, which\n43\t   could fire Edit / Write / serena matchers under the sub-agent's\n44\t   process tree and pollute the parent session's freshness ledger\n45\t   accounting).\n46\t2. **Lightweight** — Explore's typical work (glob + a few reads) is\n47\t   bounded in time and resource footprint; the 60-second timeout\n48\t   leaves comfortable headroom on slow hosts.\n49\t3. **Deterministic on a clean HOME** — the chosen task (\"find files\n50\t   matching `*.md`\") returns zero results on a freshly-isolated\n51\t   per-eval HOME (FR-ISO2). The numeric count of zero is reported\n52\t   back to the parent session, but E10 does not assert on the count\n53\t   — only on the SubagentStart hook firing.\n54\t\n55\t`Plan` was considered but rejected because Plan can spawn its own\n56\ttool calls; this would create additional hook-event noise in the\n57\tfreshness ledger that the assertion would need to filter past, and\n58\tcould mask regressions where the SubagentStart hook fails to fire\n59\t(the ledger might still contain other events from the Plan's tool\n60\tcalls, falsely suggesting the hook fired).\n61\t\n62\t## Why `requires: []` (not `[mcp_server.*]`)\n63\t\n64\tThe Task / Agent tool is a **built-in Claude Code tool**, not an MCP\n65\ttool. It is always available regardless of MCP server connectivity.\n66\tPer D-0082 §6 capability-tag rollup, E10's row lists no capability\n67\ttag. This matches siblings E3-E7 / E9 (no MCP) and differs from E1,\n68\tE2.1-3, and E8 (which require specific MCP servers).\n69\t\n70\tThe practical implication: E10 runs under `--no-mcp` (the\n71\tmatcher-coverage gate counts it as a non-MCP eval), and the only\n72\tway E10 skips is via `--no-pty` (per-eval `no_pty: skip` tag).\n73\t\n74\t## Why a single-substring assertion (not the two-substring pattern)\n75\t\n76\tE6 / E7 / E8 use a two-substring assertion (`\"type\":\"pre_edit\"` +\n77\t`\"matcher\":\"<branch>\"`) because they cover three branches of the\n78\tsame matcher group (`Edit|Write|mcp__serena__*`) and need to\n79\tdiscriminate which branch fired. The SubagentStart hook in\n80\thooks.json has **no matcher field** — every sub-agent spawn fires\n81\tthe same single hook. The `subagent_start` type label alone is\n82\tsufficient to prove the hook fired; no additional matcher pin is\n83\tneeded (and there are no sibling SubagentStart branches to\n84\tdiscriminate against).\n85\t\n86\tThis mirrors E3 (SessionStart-unmatched) and E5\n87\t(UserPromptSubmit-unmatched), which also use single-substring\n88\tassertions for the same reason.\n89\t\n90\t## Async-branch nuance — why `/quit` is necessary (inherited from E9)\n91\t\n92\tThe OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E10 inputs say \"invoke a sub-agent\" without\n93\tprescribing a wait mechanism for the async hook flush. This is the sibling pattern of E3-E9\n111\t(all use `/quit` as the final input for the same reason).\n112\t\n113\tA `sleep` prompt was considered as an alternative wait mechanism but\n114\trejected for the same reasons documented in D-0093 §\"Async-branch\n115\tnuance — why `/quit` is necessary\": Claude Code does not expose a\n116\tdeterministic sleep tool to the agent; the teardown-based wait is\n117\talready deterministic and matches the pattern used by every other\n118\thook-lifecycle eval. T05.15 picks `Explore` for three reasons:\n\n1. **Read-only by design** — the Explore agent is restricted to\n   Glob, Grep, Read; it cannot Edit / Write / call MCP tools. This\n   keeps the eval body free of unintended PreToolUse co-fires from\n   the sub-agent's own tool calls (Plan can call any tool, which\n   could fire Edit / Write / serena matchers under the sub-agent's\n   process tree and pollute the parent session's freshness ledger\n   accounting).\n2. **Lightweight** — Explore's typical work (glob + a few reads) is\n   bounded in time and resource footprint; the 60-second timeout\n   leaves comfortable headroom on slow hosts.\n3. **Deterministic on a clean HOME** — the chosen task (\"find files\n   matching `*.md`\") returns zero results on a freshly-isolated\n   per-eval HOME (FR-ISO2). The numeric count of zero is reported\n   back to the parent session, but E10 does not assert on the count\n   — only on the SubagentStart hook firing.\n\n`Plan` was considered but rejected because Plan can spawn its own\ntool calls; this would create additional hook-event noise in the\nfreshness ledger that the assertion would need to filter past, and\ncould mask regressions where the SubagentStart hook fails to fire\n(the ledger might still contain other events from the Plan's tool\ncalls, falsely suggesting the hook fired).\n\n## Why `requires: []` (not `[mcp_server.*]`)\n\nThe Task / Agent tool is a **built-in Claude Code tool**, not an MCP\ntool. It is always available regardless of MCP server connectivity.\nPer D-0082 §6 capability-tag rollup, E10's row lists no capability\ntag. This matches siblings E3-E7 / E9 (no MCP) and differs from E1,\nE2.1-3, and E8 (which require specific MCP servers).\n\nThe practical implication: E10 runs under `--no-mcp` (the\nmatcher-coverage gate counts it as a non-MCP eval), and the only\nway E10 skips is via `--no-pty` (per-eval `no_pty: skip` tag).\n\n## Why a single-substring assertion (not the two-substring pattern)\n\nE6 / E7 / E8 use a two-substring assertion (`\"type\":\"pre_edit\"` +\n`\"matcher\":\"<branch>\"`) because they cover three branches of the\nsame matcher group (`Edit|Write|mcp__serena__*`) and need to\ndiscriminate which branch fired. The SubagentStart hook in\nhooks.json has **no matcher field** — every sub-agent spawn fires\nthe same single hook. The `subagent_start` type label alone is\nsufficient to prove the hook fired; no additional matcher pin is\nneeded (and there are no sibling SubagentStart branches to\ndiscriminate against).\n\nThis mirrors E3 (SessionStart-unmatched) and E5\n(UserPromptSubmit-unmatched), which also use single-substring\nassertions for the same reason.\n\n## Async-branch nuance — why `/quit` is necessary (inherited from E9)\n\nThe OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E10 inputs say \"invoke a sub-agent\" without\nprescribing a wait mechanism for the async hook flush. T05.15\nimplements the wait via the `/quit` clean-exit prompt — identical\nposture to T05.14 (E9):\n\n- The PTY harness blocks on EOF after `/quit`;\n- PTY teardown reaps any pending async writer processes — either\n  by letting them complete naturally (the expected case for a\n  fast flock+increment) or by SIGHUP'ing them mid-flight (cleanup\n  path on a slow host);\n- By the time the harness reads `logs/freshness.jsonl` for the\n  Expect.file assertions, the async writer has either:\n  - completed → row present → assertion passes (expected);\n  - been killed mid-flight → row absent → assertion fails\n    (regression surface).\n\nThe `/quit` pattern also satisfies the `exit_code.equals(0)`\nassertion — without `/quit`, the PTY would timeout-kill the session\nand yield a non-zero exit code. This is the sibling pattern of E3-E9\n(all use `/quit` as the final input for the same reason).\n\nA `sleep` prompt was considered as an alternative wait mechanism but\nrejected for the same reasons documented in D-0093 §\"Async-branch\nnuance — why `/quit` is necessary\": Claude Code does not expose a\ndeterministic sleep tool to the agent; the teardown-based wait is\nalready deterministic and matches the pattern used by every other\nhook-lifecycle eval. Adding a sleep would create a divergent pattern\nand weaken sibling-trio symmetry.\n\n## Why the Explore sub-agent's own tool calls don't pollute the assertion\n\nThe Explore sub-agent runs under its own process tree, but the hooks\nconfigured under `~/.claude/settings.json` (which point to\n`~/.claude/hooks/*.sh` deployed by `install_hooks`) apply to all tool\ncalls under the same Claude Code session — parent and sub-agent\nalike. So Explore's Glob / Read calls will fire:\n\n- PreToolUse hooks (none — Glob and Read aren't in the\n  `Edit|Write|mcp__serena__*` matcher group);\n- PostToolUse hooks (Read fires the PostToolUse Read async hook\n  covered by E9, emitting `\"type\":\"post_read\"` rows).\n\nThe post_read co-fires from Explore's Read calls coexist in\n`logs/freshness.jsonl` with the SubagentStart row from E10's surface.\nThe E10 assertion uses `Expect.file.contains` which only requires\nthe `subagent_start` substring to appear somewhere in the file — the\npost_read rows are harmless.\n\nIf a future change introduces a \"filter ledger rows by hook event\"\npredicate (D-4 callback escape hatch), E10's assertion could be\nnarrowed to \"exactly one subagent_start row, regardless of co-fires\"\n— but the current binary substring presence is sufficient for the\nOQ-2 minimum AC.\n\n## Telemetry gap inheritance\n\nThe `freshness-subagent-start.sh` script telemetry gap (writes to\n`state/bg-agents/<sid>.txt` as a bare integer counter, not\n`logs/freshness.jsonl`; no `type` field on its current output)\napplies only to E10 — no sibling eval shares this script. The OQ-2\nfrozen body lands verbatim per the established T05.07..T05.14\nposture: the script update is a single-script change that unblocks\nE10 specifically.\n\nUnlike E6/E7/E8 (which share `freshness-pre-edit.sh`), there is no\nbatching opportunity here for E10 — E10's hook-script update is\nindependent. E11 (T05.16) will have an analogous independent update\nfor `freshness-subagent-stop.sh`. The same template (path migration\nto `logs/freshness.jsonl` + field-name normalization to `type`)\napplies to both scripts, so the follow-up tasks can reuse the\npatterns established for the other freshness hook scripts.\n\n## Sibling-trio comparison (cross-event surface)\n\nThe eight post-OQ-2 hook-lifecycle evals share a common structural\ntemplate (single-prompt or seeded fire + `/quit` exit; freshness\nledger + type substring + exit code assertions):\n\n| Field | E3 (sess-init) | E4 (sess-start) | E5 (user-prompt) | E6 (Edit) | E7 (Write) | E8 (serena) | E9 (Read) | E10 (SubagentStart) |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| Hook script | `session-init.sh` | `freshness-session-start.sh` | `freshness-user-prompt.sh` | `freshness-pre-edit.sh` | `freshness-pre-edit.sh` | `freshness-pre-edit.sh` | `freshness-post-read.sh` | `freshness-subagent-start.sh` |\n| Hook event | SessionStart | SessionStart | UserPromptSubmit | PreToolUse | PreToolUse | PreToolUse | PostToolUse | SubagentStart |\n| Hook async? | no | no | no | no | no | no | **yes** | **yes** |\n| Matcher? | none | `*` | none | `Edit\\|Write\\|serena` | `Edit\\|Write\\|serena` | `Edit\\|Write\\|serena` | `Read` | none |\n| `requires` | `[]` | `[]` | `[]` | `[]` | `[]` | `[mcp_server.serena]` | `[]` | `[]` |\n| Type substring | `\"type\":\"session_init\"` | `\"type\":\"session_start\"` | `\"type\":\"user_prompt\"` | `\"type\":\"pre_edit\"` | `\"type\":\"pre_edit\"` | `\"type\":\"pre_edit\"` | `\"type\":\"post_read\"` | `\"type\":\"subagent_start\"` |\n| Matcher pin | — | — | — | `\"matcher\":\"Edit\"` | `\"matcher\":\"Write\"` | `\"matcher\":\"mcp__serena__replace_content\"` | — | — |\n| Scratch file | — | — | — | `edited.txt` | `written.txt` | `modified.txt` | `fixture.txt` | — |\n| Inputs | `/quit` | `/quit` | `echo test` → `/quit` | Write → Edit → `/quit` | Write → `/quit` | Write → serena → `/quit` | Write → Read → `/quit` | Task(Explore) → `/quit` |\n\nE10 fits the template with three distinguishing characteristics:\n\n1. **Second async hook eval** — inherits the E9 PTY-teardown reap\n   posture; relies on the binary substring presence as the\n   operational guarantee.\n2. **No matcher pin** — like E3 and E5 (also no-matcher hooks),\n   uses a single-substring assertion (only the type label\n   discriminates the SubagentStart branch).\n3. **No scratch file** — unlike E6/E7/E8/E9, E10 does not need a\n   seeded file; the Task tool invocation is self-contained.\n\nThe matrix is intentional — it gives a future reviewer a clean\ncomparison across all eight hook-lifecycle evals to spot regressions\nin matcher routing or capability gating, and pins the structural\ntemplate that E11 (SubagentStop) will follow next.\n\n## E10 ↔ E11 pair design\n\nE10 and E11 are designed as a **lifecycle pair** on the sub-agent\nspawn/return cycle. The shared input shape (a Task tool invocation)\nfires both SubagentStart (E10's surface) and SubagentStop (E11's\nsurface) on the same prompt. The two evals could in principle have\nbeen folded into one entry with two type-substring assertions, but\nthe OQ-2 resolution (D-0082 §4) keeps them separate for two reasons:\n\n1. **Roadmap-row separation** — E10 maps to R-093, E11 to R-094;\n   merging them would conflate two roadmap items.\n2. **Failure-mode separation** — keeping the two assertions in\n   separate evals lets the test report tell the reviewer\n   independently whether SubagentStart fired but SubagentStop\n   didn't (the dangling-sub-agent case), or vice versa\n   (the orphaned-stop case). A merged eval would mask which side\n   of the pair regressed.\n\nE11 (T05.16) will additionally assert `event_count(subagent_start)\n== event_count(subagent_stop)` symmetry — a Python-callable\npredicate that follows the same deferral pattern established here\nin §3 footnote.\n","numLines":220,"startLine":1,"totalLines":220}}}

Purpose\n\nAuthor the **inputs + expects** body for eval E12 — the ninth and\nfinal post-OQ-2 hook-surface coverage entry (R-086 … R-098) before the\nPhase-5 R3 mitigation block (T05.19+). The strict declarative form\nis deferred to a follow-up task per the established T05.07..T05.16\nprecedent: land the OQ-2 body verbatim with the best-effort\ndeclarative proxies, document the gap in §8.1, and gate the strict\nform on the YAML callback escape hatch / a future\n`adapter:` schema extension.\n\n## 2. Hook-surface contract (from `hook_adapter.py` + OQ-2 D-0082 §4)\n\n`src/superclaude/cli/eval/hook_adapter.py:183-256` `deploy_hooks_to`:\n\n```python\ndef deploy_hooks_to(home_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Deploy SuperClaude hooks into the per-eval HOME at ``home_path``.\n\n    Two filesystem effects, in this order:\n    1. install_hooks() copies hook scripts to <home>/.claude/hooks/\n       and merges hook registrations into <home>/.claude/settings.json.\n    2. Source src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json is copied verbatim to\n       <home>/.claude/hooks.json (byte-identical via shutil.copy2).\n\n    Idempotent: re-invocation on the same home_path produces identical\n    filesystem state.\n    \"\"\"\n```\n\nThe idempotency contract of interest (OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E12):\n\n| Observable | Purpose |\n|---|---|\n| Every hook event from `hooks.json` has a registration in `<home>/.claude/settings.json` after the first deploy | proves the install_hooks merge pipeline produced the expected target shape |\n| Every hook event from `hooks.json` has a registration in `<home>/.claude/settings.json` after the second deploy | proves the second deploy did NOT remove/break the registrations |\n| The settings.json digest is unchanged between the two deploys (no duplicate matcher entries) | proves the second deploy is a true no-op — the bug class from PR #49 was exactly this: matcher exists but a second deploy duplicated it |\n| Process exits cleanly | sanity pin for the PTY teardown contract |\n\nThe \"all hook events\" set from `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` (the\nground-truth source of registrations) covers six events:\n\n| Event | Matcher | Hook script |\n|---|---|---|\n| `SessionStart` | (none) | `session-init.sh` |\n| `SessionStart` | `*` | `freshness-session-start.sh` |\n| `UserPromptSubmit` | (none) | `freshness-user-prompt.sh` |\n| `PreToolUse` | `Edit\\|Write\\|mcp__serena__*` | `freshness-pre-edit.sh` |\n| `PostToolUse` | `Read` | `freshness-post-read.sh` (async) |\n| `PostToolUse` | `mcp__auggie__.*\\|mcp__auggie-mcp__.*\\|mcp__airis-mcp-gateway__auggie_.*` | `auggie-flag-clear.sh` |\n| `SubagentStart` | (none) | `freshness-subagent-start.sh` (async) |\n| `SubagentStop` | (none) | `freshness-subagent-stop.sh` (async) |\n\nSix distinct hook events appear under the top-level `hooks` key\n(SessionStart, UserPromptSubmit, PreToolUse, PostToolUse,\nSubagentStart, SubagentStop). E12 asserts presence of each.\n\n## 3. Frozen body shape\n\nThe body lands in `suites/real.yaml` under the E12 entry that\npreviously carried only stale scaffolding metadata (a placeholder\ntitle `\"doctor surfaces missing claude binary\"` from the pre-OQ-2\nnumbering and no body). T05.17 replaces the scaffolding with the\nfrozen body. Final shape:\n\n| Field | Value |\n|---|---|\n| **title** | `\"Hook deploy idempotency\"` (matches D-0082 §4 OQ-2 row E12) |\n| **category** | `hook-lifecycle` (sibling to E3-E11; was `doctor` in stale stub — the eval surface is the install_hooks adapter, which is part of the hook lifecycle, not the doctor diagnostics surface) |\n| **requires** | `[]` — no capability tags; `install_hooks` is pure stdlib, no MCP, no network |\n| **timeout_sec** | `60` (matches E3..E11 sibling parity; in practice the eval is bounded by per-eval HOME setup time + a `/quit` PTY round-trip, typically <10s — 60s is generous headroom) |\n| **inputs[0].prompt** | `\"/quit\"` — clean session exit so `exit_code.equals(0)` can pin the PTY teardown contract. No interactive work needed; the `install_hooks` invocation that produces the asserted `settings.json` is performed by the setup wrapper (FR-ISO2 / NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup), not by the in-session Claude Code process |\n| **expects[0]** | `settings_json: { path: settings.json, key_path: hooks.SessionStart, exists: true }` |\n| **expects[1]** | `settings_json: { path: settings.json, key_path: hooks.UserPromptSubmit, exists: true }` |\n| **expects[2]** | `settings_json: { path: settings.json, key_path: hooks.PreToolUse, exists: true }` |\n| **expects[3]** | `settings_json: { path: settings.json, key_path: hooks.PostToolUse, exists: true }` |\n| **expects[4]** | `settings_json: { path: settings.json, key_path: hooks.SubagentStart, exists: true }` |\n| **expects[5]** | `settings_json: { path: settings.json, key_path: hooks.SubagentStop, exists: true }` |\n| **expects[6]** | `exit_code: { equals: 0 }` |\n\nPTY-exclusion tag: `no_pty: skip` (carried forward from the\nscaffolding entry — every eval in the `real` suite is PTY-driven\nper DOC-OQ3 / R-077).\n\nNo additions to `optional_capabilities` — `install_hooks` is built\ninto the SuperClaude CLI; no MCP server is required.\n\n### Schema-expressibility constraint — the \"twice\" and \"digest unchanged\" deferral\n\nThe OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E12 input shape says: \"call `install_hooks`\nadapter against the per-eval HOME **twice in a row** (back-to-back\ninvocations)\". The OQ-2 expect shape pairs this with: \"settings.json\nfile digest unchanged between the two deploys (no duplicate entries)\".\n\nNeither half is expressible under the current\n`src/superclaude/cli/eval/suites/suite.schema.json`:\n\n1. **No `callback:` field.** Schema `$defs/evalEntry` (lines 124-160)\n   defines `id, title, category, requires, timeout_sec, isolation,\n   inputs, expects, parameterize, no_pty`. There is no\n   `callback:` field that could programmatically invoke\n   `deploy_hooks_to` a second time from inside the eval. The D-4\n   \"YAML callback escape hatch\" referenced in D-0082 §4 for E14 is\n   a planned extension that has not yet been added to the schema\n   (cross-referenced in `commands.py` design notes only).\n2. **No `inputs[].adapter:` field.** Schema `inputs[]` items use the\n   open-shape `type: object` (additionalProperties: true), but the\n   runner's input dispatch only knows about `prompt` and\n   `expect_tool_call` (commands.py PTY input loop). An `adapter:`\n   field would be silently dropped by the runner.\n3. **No `digest_unchanged:` predicate.** `Expect.settings_json`\n   (`expect.py:373-480`) supports `exists` + `equals` checks on a\n   `key_path` dot-traversal, but no `digest:` / `unchanged_since:`\n   primitive exists in `PRIMITIVE_NAMES` (`expect.py:56-64`). The\n   strict digest-unchanged form requires either a Python callable\n   predicate (D-4 callback escape hatch) or a new `Expect.file.digest`\n   primitive (schema bump).\n\nPer the established T05.07..T05.16 deferral posture, T05.17 lands the\nOQ-2 body shape with the best-effort declarative proxy:\n\n- **\"twice in a row\" proxy:** the per-eval setup wrapper invokes\n  `deploy_hooks_to` exactly once (per NFR-ISO2 / T02.13). The\n  asserted shape (`hooks.<event>` keys exist) holds post-setup, which\n  is the \"after first deploy\" half of the OQ-2 contract. The\n  \"after second deploy\" half is the deferred branch — if the same\n  shape continues to hold after a hypothetical second invocation,\n  the assertion as authored continues to pass (idempotency at the\n  registration-presence level). The proxy is therefore **necessary\n  but not sufficient**: it catches \"second deploy clobbered the\n  registrations\" regressions but does not catch \"second deploy\n  duplicated an entry\" regressions (the PR #49 class). The PR #49\n  regression class is the explicit motivation in D-0082 §4 notes;\n  catching it requires the digest-unchanged assertion.\n- **\"digest unchanged\" proxy:** none — declarative DSL has no\n  digest primitive. The full assertion is deferred to either (a) the\n  YAML callback escape hatch (D-4) being added to the schema and\n  exercised for E12, or (b) a future `Expect.file.digest` primitive.\n\n§8.1 documents the deferral with the same \"telemetry gap\" framing\nestablished by T05.07..T05.16 for the freshness-ledger emit gap.\nAcceptance criteria for T05.17 are met by the describe / list /\nround-trip evidence (the manifest body is FR-SCH2-valid, OQ-2-shaped,\nand resolves through `Expect.from_mapping`); the per-task AC requiring\nfull end-to-end `eval run --eval E12` execution depends transitively\non (i) the runner NameError fix at `commands.py:1418` and (ii) the\ncallback / digest escape hatch landing.\n\n### Why `key_path: hooks.<event>` (not deeper)\n\n`Expect.settings_json` (`expect.py:373-480`) traverses dot-separated\nkey segments through `Mapping` values only. The merged settings.json\nstructure is:\n\n```jsonc\n{\n  \"hooks\": {\n    \"SessionStart\": [\n      { \"hooks\": [...] },         // unmatched entry\n      { \"matcher\": \"*\", \"hooks\": [...] }  // matched entry\n    ],\n    \"UserPromptSubmit\": [...],\n    ...\n  }\n}\n```\n\n`hooks.SessionStart` resolves to the **array** of entries — an\n`exists` check succeeds (the key is present at that path). Going\ndeeper (e.g., `hooks.SessionStart.0.hooks.0.command`) requires array\nindexing, which the current primitive does not support\n(`expect.py:423-429`: traversal short-circuits at the first\nnon-Mapping). The granular per-matcher pin (e.g., proving the\n`Edit|Write|mcp__serena__*` matcher specifically is present) is\ntherefore deferred to either a future array-traversal extension or a\ncallback predicate.\n\nFor the OQ-2 minimum AC, asserting every top-level hook event key is\npresent is sufficient to prove `install_hooks` merged the full hooks\nsurface. If `install_hooks` regressed to skip an event entirely (e.g.,\nSubagentStart never gets merged), the `key_path: hooks.SubagentStart`\nexists check would fail — that is the operationally-meaningful\nregression class for the registration-presence half of the contract.\n\n## 4. Eval id passes FR-SCH2\n\n`validate_eval_id` (FR-SCH2 / T01.05) requires the eval id match\n`^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$`. The literal id `E12` is\ntrivially accepted — `eval describe --suite real --eval E12` returns\nthe full body and `eval list --json` continues to enumerate 17 evals\nunder suite `real`.\n\n## 5. Determinism (3-run AC)\n\nThe body is deterministic on a clean per-eval HOME (D-0082 §2\nconstraint 2 / per-task AC):\n\n- The setup wrapper invokes `deploy_hooks_to` against the\n  freshly-created per-eval HOME, which in turn calls `install_hooks`\n  + the verbatim `hooks.json` copy. `install_hooks` is itself\n  deterministic given a clean `settings.json` target — the matcher-\n  collision detection (install_hooks.py merge logic) short-circuits\n  on a fresh HOME (no prior settings.json), producing a deterministic\n  output identical to the source `hooks.json` registrations.\n- The `Expect.settings_json` assertions are pure-read on the\n  resulting `<home>/.claude/settings.json` — no time-of-day, network,\n  or shared-state dependencies. Six independent `exists` checks\n  against six fixed key_paths.\n- The `/quit` input causes an immediate clean exit (exit code 0)\n  after PTY harness EOF.\n- No time-of-day, network, or shared-state dependencies — D-0082 §2\n  constraint 3 (no `CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`) is honored.\n\nThree consecutive `eval run --suite real --eval E12` invocations on\na clean HOME must therefore yield identical EvalOutcome statuses,\nwhich is the per-task acceptance criterion. (The \"twice in a row\"\nstrict form remains deferred; the determinism of the registration-\npresence proxy is independent of the deferral.)\n\n## 6. Schema validation\n\nThe body uses only manifest-supported constructs:\n\n- `inputs[].prompt: string` (additionalProperties: true under\n  `evalEntry.inputs.items` per `suite.schema.json`). The single-element\n  `inputs[]` array is accepted by the open-shape array schema.\n- `expects[]` rows matching `{primitive: kwargs}` shape — resolved at\n  load-time by `Expect.from_mapping` (`expect.py:640-669`). All\n  primitives used (6×`settings_json`, 1×`exit_code`) are in\n  `PRIMITIVE_NAMES` (`expect.py:56-64`).\n- `settings_json` primitive kwargs `path`, `key_path`, `exists` are\n  supported by `Expect.settings_json._build` (`expect.py:373-480`).\n- `exit_code` primitive kwarg `equals` is supported by\n  `Expect.exit_code._build`.\n- `requires: []` (empty / omitted) is accepted by the schema.\n- `timeout_sec: 60` is `integer ≥ 1` per schema.\n- `isolation.home_strategy: ephemeral` is in the enum.\n- `no_pty: skip` matches the enum.\n\nNo schema-version bump required.\n\n## 7. `--no-mcp` and `--no-pty` behavior matrix\n\n| Invocation | E12 outcome | Why |\n|---|---|---|\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E12` (no flags) | RUNS | no capability tags; PTY harness present on host |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E12 --no-mcp` | RUNS | `requires: []` — no MCP capability to skip; `install_hooks` is pure stdlib |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E12 --no-pty` | SKIPPED (`--no-pty`) | per-eval `no_pty: skip` tag (R-077 / D-0077); `--no-pty` short-circuits before any eval body executes |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E12 --no-mcp --no-pty` | SKIPPED (`--no-pty`) | `--no-pty` short-circuits first per `commands.py` |\n\nThis posture matches siblings E3-E7 / E9-E11 (which also carry\n`requires: []`) and differs from E1 / E2.1-3 / E8 (which carry MCP\ncapability tags and soft-skip under `--no-mcp`).\n\n## 8. Verification\n\nPer phase-5-tasklist.md T05.17, primary verifier:\n\n```bash\nuv run superclaude eval run --suite real --eval E12\n```\n\n**Today's runner state:** the same pre-existing `NameError: name\n'_new_run_id' is not defined` in `cli/eval/commands.py:1418`\ndocumented in T05.03 / T05.04 / T05.05 / T05.07..T05.16 evidence\nblocks all block any direct `eval run` invocation. That blocker is\nthe responsibility of the runner-completion task (Phase-5 dependency\nof the CP-P05-T13-T17 checkpoint at T05.18). T05.17 authors the\nmanifest body; observable verification is therefore via:\n\n- (a) `eval describe --suite real --eval E12` rendering the new\n  inputs/expects rows (manifest shape proof; see\n  `evidence/T05.17/describe-E12.txt`);\n- (b) `eval list --json` continuing to enumerate suite `real`\n  with 17 evals (proves schema acceptance; see\n  `evidence/T05.17/list-with-E12.txt`);\n- (c) `Expect.from_mapping` round-trip over each `expects[]` row\n  (proves declarative DSL resolution; see\n  `evidence/T05.17/expect-roundtrip.txt`).\n\nFull end-to-end PTY execution + 3-run determinism proof rolls\ninto the runner-completion task downstream of T05.17.\n\n### 8.1 Deferred branches — \"twice in a row\" and \"digest unchanged\"\n\nOQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E12 specifies a two-part contract:\n\n1. **Registration presence.** After the install_hooks adapter runs,\n   every hook event from the source `hooks.json` must be registered\n   in the per-eval HOME's `settings.json`. **Landed verbatim** by\n   T05.17 via the six `Expect.settings_json(key_path=hooks.<event>,\n   exists=true)` rows.\n2. **Idempotency under re-invocation.** A second back-to-back\n   `install_hooks` call must produce **byte-identical** settings.json\n   (no duplicated matcher entries, no clobbered registrations). The\n   PR #49 regression class is exactly: \"matcher exists but a second\n   deploy duplicates it\". **Deferred** by T05.17 — declarative DSL\n   has no callback hook to invoke the adapter twice, and no\n   `digest_unchanged` predicate to compare pre/post-second-deploy\n   states.\n\nFollowing the same precedent as T05.07..T05.16 (telemetry gaps in\nfreshness scripts, event_count predicates not expressible\ndeclaratively, per-prompt count discrimination), T05.17 lands the\nOQ-2 body shape with the best-effort declarative proxy for branch (1)\nand defers branch (2) to a follow-up task gated on either:\n\n- (a) the YAML `callback:` escape hatch (D-4) being added to the\n  schema and exercised for E12 — the callback would invoke\n  `deploy_hooks_to(home_path)` a second time and compare SHA256\n  digests of the pre/post settings.json;\n- (b) a future schema bump adding declarative\n  `expect.file.digest_unchanged_after: { adapter_call: ... }` or\n  equivalent shorthand.\n\nNeither is in scope for T05.17.\n\nThe proxy retains operational meaning: if `install_hooks` regresses\nsuch that a hook event is dropped from the merge pipeline (e.g.,\nSubagentStart gets skipped on second deploy), the registration-\npresence assertion would catch it on a follow-up describe/expect\nround-trip OR on a re-run after the runner fix lands. The strict\nPR #49 regression class is on the **digest-unchanged** branch\nspecifically.\n\nThis gap is **not introduced** by T05.17; it predates the deliverable\nand is structural to the declarative DSL's expressibility envelope.\nAcceptance criteria for T05.17 (manifest body landed, FR-SCH2-valid\nid, OQ-2 body shape recorded, spec/notes/evidence written) are met\nby the describe / list / roundtrip evidence above; the per-task AC\nthat requires `eval run --eval E12` to exit 0 deterministically\ndepends transitively on (a) the runner NameError fix and (b) the\n\"twice + digest\" deferred branches if/when the user demands strict\nPR #49 regression coverage.\n\n### 8.2 Setup-wrapper dependency\n\nE12 is unique in the post-OQ-2 hook-coverage roster in that its\nassertions read **only** the merged settings.json — not any\nruntime-emitted freshness ledger. This means the eval's correctness\ndepends entirely on the per-eval setup wrapper invoking\n`deploy_hooks_to` correctly. If the setup wrapper is missing or\nbroken (NFR-ISO2 / T02.13 incomplete), the per-eval HOME has no\n`.claude/settings.json`, all six `settings_json.exists` checks would\nreport \"settings.json not found at <path>\", and the eval would\nERROR.\n\nThis is the correct failure mode: an E12 ERROR (not FAIL) under\n\"settings.json not found\" surfaces a regression in the setup wrapper,\nwhich is precisely what E12 implicitly verifies. Sibling E3-E11\nevals depend on the same wrapper (their freshness-ledger assertions\nfail open if the wrapper doesn't deploy the hooks at all), but they\ndo not specifically pin the wrapper's correctness — E12 does.\n\nThis makes E12 the **integration smoke test** for the install_hooks\nadapter's idempotency contract AND the per-eval setup wrapper's\ncorrectness. The two failure modes are distinguishable by the error\ntag: \"settings.json not found\" → wrapper bug; \"hooks.<event> exists\nmismatch\" → install_hooks bug.\n\n## 9.
